\displaystyle|(p_{\triangleright(b)}-p_{\triangleright(a)})-(p_{b}-p_{a})|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{4}r^{-1},

(6.5)

\displaystyle|p_{a}-p_{b}|

\displaystyle\leq N^{6}r(t_{\triangleright(a)}-t_{a})^{-1}.

Proof.

First observe that (
6.4
) is a direct consequence of
the fact that
\bm{\Gamma}
is
N^{4}
-complete.

The bounds (
6.3
) and (
6.5
) are less trivial.
Now we prove (
6.3
). By Corollary
6.2
,

|(y_{\triangleright(a)}-y_{a})-(t_{\triangleright(a)}-t_{a})p_{a}|\leq\alpha N^{6}r.

On the other hand,
|y_{\triangleright(a)}-y_{\triangleright(b)}|\leq 4r
and
|y_{a}-y_{b}|\leq 4r
, so also

|(y_{\triangleright(a)}-y_{a})-(t_{\triangleright(b)}-t_{b})p_{b}|\leq 2\alpha N^{6}r.

It follows that

(6.6)

|(t_{\triangleright(a)}-t_{a})p_{a}-(t_{\triangleright(b)}-t_{b})p_{b}|\leq 3\alpha N^{6}r.

In particular, using
|u-v|\geq||u|-|v||
for any vectors
u,v\in{\mathbf{R}}^{d}
,
we have

|(t_{\triangleright(a)}-t_{a})|p_{a}|-(t_{\triangleright(b)}-t_{b})|p_{b}||\leq 3\alpha N^{6}r.

By the conservation of kinetic energy condition,
||p_{a}|-|p_{b}||\leq C\alpha Nr^{-1}
.
Therefore

(t_{\triangleright(b)}-t_{b})||p_{a}|-|p_{b}||\leq C\alpha N^{2}r^{-1}\tau\leq C\alpha N^{2}r,

so we have

|p_{a}||(t_{\triangleright(a)}-t_{a})-(t_{\triangleright(b)}-t_{b})|\leq 4\alpha N^{6}r.

Dividing through by
|p_{a}|\geq\frac{1}{2}|p_{1,0}|
finishes the proof of (
6.3
).

The remaining nontrivial bound is (
6.5
). We obtain this
from (
6.6
) after first replacing
t_{\triangleright(b)}-t_{b}
by
t_{\triangleright(a)}-t_{a}

using (
6.3
) to obtain

(t_{\triangleright(a)}-t_{a})|p_{a}-p_{b}|\leq C\alpha N^{6}r.

Dividing through by
t_{\triangleright(a)}-t_{a}
finishes the result.
∎

Recall that for a phase space point
\xi=(x,p)\in{{\mathbf{R}}^{2d}}
, we define the free evolution
U_{s}(\xi)
,
also written simply
\xi_{s}
, by

U_{s}((x,p)):=(x+sp,p).

Definition 6.18

(Twinning in phase space)
.

We say that two points
\xi,\eta\in{{\mathbf{R}}^{2d}}
are
(\beta,s)
-twinned if

d_{r}(\xi,U_{s}(\eta))\leq\beta
.

A corollary of Lemma
6.17
is that the phase space endpoints of a long ladder must be twinned.

Lemma 6.19
.

Suppose that
\bm{\Gamma}
is an
N^{4}
-complete path and the restriction of the
partition to the pair of intervals

[a,a^{\prime}]_{K^{+}(\Gamma^{+})}\cup[b,b^{\prime}]_{K^{-}(\Gamma^{-})}
forms a renormalized ladder partition
so that in particular
\{a,b\}\in P(\bm{\Gamma})
and
\{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\}\in P(\bm{\Gamma})
.
Suppose moreover that
\ell(a^{\prime})-\ell(a)>1
.

Then for some
s\in{\mathbf{R}}
,
\xi_{2\ell(a)-1}
is
s
-twinned to
\xi_{2\ell(b)-1}
and

\xi_{2\ell(a^{\prime})-2}
is
(N^{10},s)
-twinned to
\xi_{2\ell(b^{\prime})-2}
.

Proof.

Because there are at most
k_{max}
collisions in a segment, there must exist a pair of
consecutive external collisions
c,c^{\prime}\in[a,a^{\prime}]
that are paired with consecutive
collisions
d,d^{\prime}\in[b,b^{\prime}]
and which satisfy
|t_{c}-t_{c^{\prime}}|\geq k_{max}^{-1}\tau
.
Applying Lemma
6.17
we therefore conclude that
|p_{c}-p_{d}|\leq N^{8}r^{-1}
.
Then iteratively applying the bound (
6.4
) we conclude
that
|p_{a}-p_{b}|\leq 2N^{8}r^{-1}
and
|p_{a^{\prime}}-p_{b^{\prime}}|\leq 2N^{8}r^{-1}
.

∎

7.
Skeletons and diagrams

One way to apply Lemma
6.16
is to decompose the path integral
according to the data

(E^{rec}(\bm{\Gamma}),E^{tube}(\bm{\Gamma}),E^{cone}(\bm{\Gamma}),G_{C}(\bm{\Gamma}),G_{A}(\bm{\Gamma})),

where
G_{A}
is the graph of atypical collisions,

G_{A}(\bm{\Gamma}):=\{(a,b)\in G(\bm{\Gamma})\mid a\not\in\Typical(\bm{\Gamma})\text{ or }b\not\in\Typical(\bm{\Gamma})\}.

Within each piece, the information about the events and the graphs
G_{C}
and
G_{A}
about places a strong
constraint on
P(\bm{\Gamma})
by Lemma
6.16
and therefore one can hope to split
up the expectation

{\mathbf{E}\,}O_{\Gamma^{-}}^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{+}}
in a nice way. The problem with this approach is that there are simply
too many graphs to sum over. As an extreme example, suppose that the graph
C(\bm{\Gamma})
is connected.
This places a strong constraint on every single collision in
\Gamma
, so one can hope that the contribution
of such paths is on the order
{\varepsilon}^{cN}
. On the other hand, there are
2^{\Theta(N^{2})}
connected paths
on
CN
collisions, and the combinatorial factor
2^{N^{2}}
swamps the factor of
{\varepsilon}^{cN}
.

We therefore need a more parsimonious way of recording enough information about the geometry of
\bm{\Gamma}

that does not contain too much information about
\bm{\Gamma}
and which lends itself to a decomposition of
the sum.

The approach we take in this paper is
to record minimal spanning forests of the graphs
E^{rec}(\bm{\Gamma})
,
E^{tube}(\bm{\Gamma})
,

E^{cone}(\bm{\Gamma})
,
G_{C}(\bm{\Gamma})
, and
G_{A}(\bm{\Gamma})
. To be more precise, let

{\mathbf{G}}_{all}:=\binom{{\mathbf{K}}_{all}}{2}
be the set of all pairs of distinct collision indices
in
{\mathbf{K}}_{all}
. Let
{\mathbf{G}}^{\pm}\subset E({\mathbf{K}}_{all})
be the set of pairs
(a,b)
with

\sign(a)\not=\sign(b)
(so
{\mathbf{G}}_{all}
is the complete graph on
{\mathbf{K}}_{all}
, and

{\mathbf{G}}^{\pm}
is a complete bipartite graph).
We fix an arbitrary assignment of weights
w:{\mathbf{G}}_{all}\to{\mathbf{R}}^{+}

to each edge so that each edge has a distinct weight, and so that
w(e)>w(e^{\prime})
whenever

e\in{\mathbf{G}}^{\pm}
and
e^{\prime}\not\in{\mathbf{G}}^{\pm}
.

Then, given a graph
G\subset{\mathbf{G}}_{all}
, we define the minimal spanning forest
F_{G}
to be the graph
of minimal total weight which has the same connected components as
G
.

Before we define the skeleton of a graph, we record a slight modification to the graph
G(\bm{\Gamma})
that we
will use. We redefine

\displaystyle G(\bm{\Gamma})=\{(a,b)\in E({\mathbf{K}}(\Gamma))\mid|y_{a}-y_{b}|\leq 2r\text{ and }\sign(a)\not=\sign(b)\text{ or }|y_{a}-y_{b}|\leq 4r\}.

The utility of this new definition is that, if
(a,b),(a,c)\in G(\bm{\Gamma})\cap E^{\pm}
,
then
(b,c)\in G(\bm{\Gamma})
. This, along with the dominance of the weights
of the edge set
E^{\pm}
, ensure that the minimal forest
F_{G}(\bm{\Gamma})
has the property
that no two edges in
F_{G}(\bm{\Gamma})\cap E^{\pm}
are incident to a vertex. We also note that this
modification of the definition of
G(\bm{\Gamma})
, though it may change the cluster graph and therefore
the set of typical indices, otherwise does not affect the conclusions of Lemma
6.16
.

Definition 7.1

(Geometric skeleton)
.

The skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}(\bm{\Gamma})
of a path
\bm{\Gamma}
consists of the
tuple of forests
(F_{rec}(\bm{\Gamma}),F_{tube}(\bm{\Gamma}),F_{cone}(\bm{\Gamma}),F_{C}(\bm{\Gamma}),F_{A}(\bm{\Gamma})

The
complexity
of the skeleton

{\mathcal{F}}
, written
\|{\mathcal{F}}\|

is given by
\|{\mathcal{F}}\|:=|F_{rec}|+|F_{tube}|+|F_{cone}|+|F_{C}|+|F_{A}|
,
and the
support

\supp{\mathcal{F}}(\bm{\Gamma})\subset{\mathbf{K}}_{all}

of the skeleton is the set of collision indices contained in an edge in

F_{rec}(\bm{\Gamma})\cup F_{tube}(\bm{\Gamma})\cup F_{cone}(\bm{\Gamma})\cup F_{C}(\bm{\Gamma})\cup F_{A}(\bm{\Gamma})
.

An important combinatorial feature of the cluster forest is that it cannot have too many
edges in
E^{\pm}
. More precisely, the number of edges in
F_{C}\cap E^{\pm}
is
controlled by
|F_{C}|
.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
F_{C}
and
F_{A}
be the cluster forest and atypical forest (respectively)
of some path
\bm{\Gamma}
, and let

S_{AC}\subset{\mathbf{K}}_{all}
be the set of collision indices that are contained in an
edge of
(F_{C}\cup F_{A})\cap E({\mathbf{K}}_{all})\setminus E^{\pm}
. Then

|(F_{C}\cup F_{A})\cap E^{\pm}|\leq 2|S_{R}|.

Proof.

This follows from the observation above that no two edges in
F_{C}\cap E^{\pm}

or
F_{A}\cap E^{\pm}
are incident to any vertex.
∎

Lemma
6.16
determines the structure of the partition
P(\bm{\Gamma})

for the typical indices, which are those not contained in the support
\supp{\mathcal{F}}(\bm{\Gamma})
.
Fortunately the partition
P(\bm{\Gamma})|_{\supp{\mathcal{F}}(\bm{\Gamma})}
is exactly the connected
components of the graph
F_{C}\cup F_{R}
. In particular, we have the following result.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
\bm{\Gamma}
be an extended path with skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}
. Then

\supp{\mathcal{F}}=K(\bm{\Gamma})\setminus\Typical(\bm{\Gamma})
, and
P(\bm{\Gamma})

is a refinement of the partition
\{\supp{\mathcal{F}},\Typical(\bm{\Gamma})\}

(that is,
P(\bm{\Gamma})
saturates
\supp{\mathcal{F}}
).

Moreover, if
\bm{\Gamma}^{\prime}
is another extended path with
{\mathcal{F}}(\bm{\Gamma}^{\prime})={\mathcal{F}}
,
then
P(\bm{\Gamma})|_{\supp{\mathcal{F}}}=P(\bm{\Gamma}^{\prime})|_{\supp{\mathcal{F}}}
.

We write
P_{{\mathcal{F}}}
for the partition
P(\bm{\Gamma})|_{\supp{\mathcal{F}}}
.

7.1.
Decomposing paths using skeletons

We will decompose
the integral over
\Omega_{ext}^{N}\times\Omega_{ext}^{N}
according to the skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}(\bm{\Gamma})
.
Let
{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}:\Omega_{\alpha,S}^{N}\times\Omega_{\alpha,S}^{N}\to\{0,1\}

be the indicator function of the set of paths
\bm{\Gamma}
with skeleton exactly
{\mathcal{F}}
.
We can use these indicator functions to partition the domain of integration of the path integral
as follows:

(7.1)

\Xi(\bm{\Gamma}){\mathbf{1}}(\Gamma^{+}\sim\Gamma^{-}){\mathbf{E}\,}O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{-}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{+}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{-}\\
=\sum_{{\mathcal{F}}}\iint\Xi(\bm{\Gamma}){\mathbf{1}}(\Gamma^{+}\sim\Gamma^{-}){\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}(\bm{\Gamma}){\mathbf{E}\,}O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{-}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}.

In the next section we will see how to use the structure of the
P(\bm{\Gamma})

to write down a more useful expression for
{\mathbf{E}\,}O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{-}}
, which is the point
of decomposing the integral as a sum over skeletons. The cost of this manipulation is that we are
left with the indicator function
{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}(\bm{\Gamma})
, which is difficult to work with because it
depends on all of the path variables at the same time. For example, the indicator function

{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
includes many “negative constraints” that ensure that collisions belong to different
components of
F_{R}

do not
form a recollision. Fortunately the following lemma, which is simply
an abstraction of the identity (
B.3
)
proven in Appendix
B
, provides a more convenient expression for the function

{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Lemma 7.4
.

For any graph
G\subset\binom{[m]}{2}
, the indicator function for
the minimal forest
F_{G}
of
G
being equal to
F
has the representation

{\mathbf{1}}(F_{G}=F)=\sum_{F^{\prime}\supset F}\chi_{F,F^{\prime}}(G),

where the sum is over forests
F^{\prime}
containing
F
. In
addition the functions
\chi_{F,F^{\prime}}
have a
structure in terms of the connected components
P_{F^{\prime}}
of
F^{\prime}
,
in the sense that

\chi_{F,F^{\prime}}(G)=\prod_{S\in P_{F^{\prime}}}\chi_{F,F^{\prime},S}(G_{S}).

Here
G_{S}
is the induced subgraph on the vertex set
S
.
Moreover,
|\chi_{F,F^{\prime}}(G)|\leq 1
for any
F,F^{\prime},G
and

\supp\chi_{F,F^{\prime}}(G)\subset\{G\mid G\supset F^{\prime}\}
.
Finally, the function
\chi_{\varnothing,\varnothing}=1
is the constant function.

We will apply Lemma
7.4
to write

{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathcal{F}}=\sum_{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\supset{\mathcal{F}}}\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\bm{\Gamma})
.
The only addiitonal observation we need is that the forests

F_{E^{rec}(\bm{\Gamma})}
,

F_{E^{tube}(\bm{\Gamma})}
,

F_{E^{cone}(\bm{\Gamma})}
, and

F_{C(\bm{\Gamma})}
are determined only using edge indicator functions

{\mathbf{1}}((a,b)\in E^{rec}(\bm{\Gamma}))
,

{\mathbf{1}}((a,b)\in E^{tube}(\bm{\Gamma}))
,

{\mathbf{1}}((a,b)\in E^{cone}(\bm{\Gamma}))
,

{\mathbf{1}}((a,b)\in G(\bm{\Gamma}))
, which are each functions only of the
path variables
((p_{a-1},p_{a},y_{a}),(p_{b-1},p_{b},y_{b}))
.

Lemma 7.5
.

The indicator function
{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}(\bm{\Gamma})
can be written as a sum

{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}(\bm{\Gamma})=\sum_{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\geq{\mathcal{F}}}\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\bm{\Gamma}),

where each function
\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}
depends only on the variables in the
support
\supp{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
and is bounded by the indicator function
{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\bm{\Gamma})
,

\sup_{\bm{\Gamma}}|\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\bm{\Gamma})|\leq{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\bm{\Gamma}).

Moreover,
\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}
has the partial product structure

(7.2)

\begin{split}\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\bm{\Gamma})=(-1)^{c({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}&\chi^{+}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{+})\chi^{-}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{-})\chi^{clust}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{y}}_{\supp(F_{C^{\prime}}\cup F_{A^{\prime}})}),\end{split}

where
{\mathbf{y}}_{\supp(F_{C^{\prime}}\cup F_{A^{\prime}})}
is the tuple of the values of
y_{a}
for

a\in\supp F_{C^{\prime}}\cup F_{A^{\prime}}
, and
\chi^{clust}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
obeys the bound

\chi^{clust}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{y}}_{\supp F_{C^{\prime}}\cup F_{A^{\prime}}})\leq\prod_{a\sim_{F_{C^{\prime}}}b}{\mathbf{1}}(|y_{a}-y_{b}|\leq 4Nr)\prod_{a\sim_{F_{A^{\prime}}}b}{\mathbf{1}}(|y_{a}-y_{b}|\leq 4Nr).

To estimate the contribution of the diagrams we will need to express

\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}
as a mixture of
functions with a product structure.
The term that doesn’t split is the function
\chi^{clust}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}
, which depends
on the variables
{\mathbf{y}}_{\supp F_{C^{\prime}}\cup\supp F_{A^{\prime}}}
. To keep track of the variables
affected by the cutoff
\chi^{clust}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}
we introduce the sets

J_{clust}^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})

J_{clust}^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}):=(\supp F_{C^{\prime}}\cup\supp F_{A^{\prime}})\cap{\mathbf{K}}^{\pm}_{all}.

Then, for a tuple of variables
Y^{\pm}=\{y_{a}\}_{a\in J_{clust}^{\pm}}
, which we consider
as a function
Y:J_{clust}^{pm}\to{\mathbf{R}}^{d}
and we write
\supp Y
to mean the domain of this
function. Then given any such labelled tuple of positions
Y
we define
we write

(7.3)

\chi^{\pm,Y}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma):=\chi^{\pm}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma)\delta({\mathbf{y}}_{\supp Y}(\Gamma)-Y).

This allows us to decompose
\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\bm{\Gamma})
as an integral
of functions that do split as a product of functions of
\Gamma^{+}
and
\Gamma^{-}
alone:

(7.4)

\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\bm{\Gamma})=\int\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}^{+,Y^{+}}(\Gamma^{+})\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}^{-,Y^{-}}(\Gamma^{-})\chi^{clust}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(Y^{+},Y^{-})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Y^{+}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Y^{-},

where the integral is over tuples
Y^{+}:J^{+}_{clust}\to{\mathbf{R}}^{d}

and
Y^{-}:J^{-}_{clust}\to{\mathbf{R}}^{d}
.

7.2.
The partitions associated to a skeleton

Using a skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}
we define two collections of partitions. The first is the collection
of partitions that
actually
arise as
P(\bm{\Gamma})
for some
N^{4}
-complete extended
path
\bm{\Gamma}
.

{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{F}}}=\{P(\bm{\Gamma})\mid{\mathcal{F}}(\bm{\Gamma})={\mathcal{F}}\text{ and }\bm{\Gamma}\text{ is }N^{4}-\text{complete}\}.

The second collection is of partitions that match the criteria of
Lemma
6.16
and the constraint
P|_{\supp{\mathcal{F}}}=P_{{\mathcal{F}}}
.

To define this collection of partitions it is useful to define an abstraction of the maximal atypical
interval set
{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{\Gamma})
that does not depend on the path
\bm{\Gamma}
directly.
Recall that
{\mathcal{I}}(\bm{\Gamma})
is the set of maximal atypical intervals of the form

(a,b)_{K(\bm{\Gamma})}
, which are subsets of
K(\bm{\Gamma})
. It is useful
to define an abstract interval collection which only contains the
information about the endpoints of the intervals, and is determined only by the set of
atypical collision indices.

Definition 7.6

(Abstract interval collections)
.

Let
S\subset{\mathbf{K}}_{all}
be a set of collision indices. We define the abstract interval
collection
{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(S)
to be the set of all pairs

{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(S):=\{(a,b)\in(S\cup\{0,\infty\})^{2}\mid a<b\text{ and there does not exist }c\in S\text{ such that }a<c<b\}.

Given a collision index set
K\subset{\mathbf{K}}_{all}
containing
S

and an abstract interval
I=(a,b)\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(S)
, we define the realization

I_{K}=(a,b)_{K}:=\{c\in K\mid a<c<b\}.

We can now define the set of partitions canonically associated to a skeleton.

Definition 7.7

(Canonical partition collection)
.

Given a skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}
, we define the canonical collection of partitions

{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{F}}
to be any partition
P\in{\mathcal{P}}(K)
on a collision index set

K
containing
\supp{\mathcal{F}}
such that

•

P|_{\supp{\mathcal{F}}}=P_{{\mathcal{F}}}
.

•

For every
I\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})
, either
P
saturates
I_{K}
and

P|_{I_{K}}
is a simple partition, or there exists
I^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})

such that
\sign(I)=-\sign(I^{\prime})
,
P
saturates
I_{K}\cup I^{\prime}_{K}
, and
the restriction
P|_{I_{K}\cup I^{\prime}_{K}}
is a generalized ladder.

•

If
(a,a+1)\in P
is an immediate recollision, then
\ell(a)=\ell(a+1)
.

The last condition comes from the fact that recollisions must occur within a time of

N|p|^{-1}r\ll{\varepsilon}^{-1.5}
, which is the guaranteed time of free evolution between segments.

A restatement of Lemma
6.16
in terms of the collection
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{F}}}

and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{F}}
is the statement

(7.5)

{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{F}}.

That is, every partition of the form
P(\bm{\Gamma})
for an
N^{4}
-complete path
with skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}(\bm{\Gamma})
belongs to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
.

We conclude this section by showing that the expectation

{\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{a\in K(\bm{\Gamma})}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a}).

splits as a sum over all partitions in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
\bm{\Gamma}
be an
N^{4}
-complete path with skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}
. Then

(7.6)

\begin{split}{\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{a\in K(\bm{\Gamma})}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a})&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in{\mathcal{Q^{\prime}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}\\
\supp P=K(\bm{\Gamma})\end{subarray}}\prod_{S\in P}{\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{a\in S}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a})\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}\\
\supp P=K(\bm{\Gamma})\end{subarray}}\prod_{S\in P}{\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{a\in S}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a}).\end{split}

Note that in this sum, we only consider partitions of the index set
K(\bm{\Gamma})
, which
is indicated by the support constraint
\supp P=K(\bm{\Gamma})
.

The key observation is the following incompatibility of diagrams in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
P,P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{F}}
be two partitions in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}

with
\supp P=\supp P^{\prime}
. Then either there exists sets
S\in P
and
S^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}
such that

S\cap S^{\prime}
is a singleton set, or
P=P^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Suppose
P\not=P^{\prime}
, so that there exist sets
S\in P
and
S^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}

such that
S\not=S^{\prime}
and
S\cap S^{\prime}\not=\varnothing
.
If
|S|>2
, then
S
is a cluster, and is therefore specified by
{\mathcal{F}}(\bm{\Gamma})
. Thus
it follows that
|S|=|S^{\prime}|=2
. Since
0<|S\cap S^{\prime}|<|S|
, it follows that
|S\cap S^{\prime}|=1
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.8
.

Since
\bm{\Gamma}
is
N^{4}
-complete and

{\mathcal{F}}(\bm{\Gamma})={\mathcal{F}}
it follows by (
7.5
)
that
P(\bm{\Gamma})\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
.
Thus for
P=P(\bm{\Gamma})
, we have

{\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{a\in K(\bm{\Gamma})}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a})=\prod_{S\in P}{\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{a\in S}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a}).

Now suppose that
P\not=P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{F}}
and
\supp P=\supp P^{\prime}
. We will show that

\prod_{S^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}}{\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{a\in S^{\prime}}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a})=0

Indeed, by Lemma
7.9
, there is some
S^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}
and
S\in P

such that
S\cap S^{\prime}=\{a_{0}\}
. This collision site
a_{0}
is separated from any other
collision in
S^{\prime}
, so

{\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{a\in S^{\prime}}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a})={\mathbf{E}\,}{\widehat{V_{y_{a_{0}}}}}(q_{a_{0}}){\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{a\in S^{\prime}\setminus\{a_{0}\}}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a})=0,

since
{\mathbf{E}\,}V=0
.
∎

8.
Colored operators

In this section we use combinatorial features of the partition set
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{F}}
to write
different expressions for (
7.6
). The key idea is the notion of a

colored operator
, which is a path operator with a correlation structure specified by a coloring
function
\psi
.

To define a colored operator, let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a fixed countable collection of colors, and
let
\{V^{(c)}\}_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}}
be a collection of independently sampled potentials
V^{(c)}
,
and let
c_{imm}\in{\mathcal{C}}
be a special color reserved for immediate recollisions.
We write
{\mathcal{C}}_{ext}={\mathcal{C}}\setminus\{c_{imm}\}
for the remaining colors in
{\mathcal{C}}
.

We say that a coloring
\psi:K\to{\mathcal{C}}

on a collision index set
K
is
valid
if
\psi^{-1}(c_{imm})
can be partitioned into
consecutive pairs,

\psi^{-1}(c_{imm})=\{(a,a+_{K}1)\}_{a\in A_{imm}(\psi)},

where
A_{imm}(\psi)
is the set of first elements of each such pair and
\ell(a)=\ell(a+1)
for
each such pair (so that recollisions cannot cross segment boundaries).

Then, given a coloring function
\psi:[1,k]\to{\mathcal{C}}
and a path
\omega\in\Omega_{k}
,
we define the colored operator

(8.1)

O_{\omega}^{\psi}:=\mathinner{|{p_{0}}\rangle}\mathinner{\langle{p_{k}}|}e^{i\varphi(\omega)}\prod_{a\in A_{imm}(\psi)}{\mathbf{E}\,}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(p_{a}-p_{a-1}){\widehat{V_{y_{a+1}}}}(p_{a+1}-p_{a}){\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{\psi(j)\not=c_{imm}}{\widehat{V_{y_{j}}^{(\psi(j))}}}(p_{j}-p_{j-1}).

We then extend this definition to extended paths
\Gamma
and colorings
\psi:K(\Gamma)\to{\mathcal{C}}

by taking a product. Given
\psi:K(\Gamma)\to{\mathcal{C}}
,
let
\psi_{\ell}
be the restriction of the coloring
\psi
to the collisions with index

(\ell,j)
for some
j
.

(8.2)

O_{\Gamma}^{\psi}=\mathinner{|{\xi_{0}}\rangle}\mathinner{\langle{\xi_{2N-1}}|}\prod_{\ell=1}^{N}\mathinner{\langle{\xi_{2\ell-2}|O_{\omega_{\ell}}^{\psi_{\ell}}|\xi_{2\ell-1}}\rangle}\prod_{\ell=1}^{N-1}\mathinner{\langle{\xi_{2\ell-1}|\xi_{2\ell}}\rangle}

8.1.
Partitions from colorings

In this section we relate expectations involving colored operators to expectations that are split
according to a partition.
Let
\psi^{+}:K^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}
and
\psi^{-}:K^{-}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be colorings on collision index sets
K^{+}

and
K^{-}
. We then define the coloring
\psi^{+}\oplus\psi^{-}:K^{+}\sqcup K^{-}\to{\mathcal{C}}
to have
restriction
\psi^{+}
on
K^{+}
and
\psi^{-}
on
K^{-}
.

Definition 8.1
.

Let
\psi^{+}
and
\psi^{-}
as above. The partition
P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})\in{\mathcal{P}}(K^{+}\sqcup K^{-})

is defined so that
a\sim_{P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})}b
if either

\psi(a)=\psi(b)\in{\mathcal{C}}_{ext}
or

b=a+1
and
a\in A_{imm}(\psi)
.

Given sets
\Psi^{+}
and
\Psi^{-}
of colorings, we write
{\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{+},\Psi^{-})
for the
set of all partitions
P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})
with
\psi^{+}\in\Psi^{+}
and
\psi^{-}\in\Psi^{-}
,
excluding partitions containing singleton cells,

{\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{+},\Psi^{-}):=\{P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})\mid\psi^{+}\in\Psi^{+},\psi^{-}\in\Psi^{-},\text{ and for every }S\in P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-}),|S|\geq 2\}.

For convenience we also write
{\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi)={\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi,\Psi)
.

The following identity is then clear from the definition that for deterministic operators
A
,

{\mathbf{E}\,}(O^{\psi^{+}}_{\Gamma^{+}})^{*}AO^{\psi^{-}}_{\Gamma^{-}}={\mathbf{E}\,}_{P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})}O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{-}},

where the latter expectation is split according to the partition
P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})
.
The identity written above is only formal in the sense that
{\mathbf{E}\,}_{P}
is not a well-defined
operation, but what is meant is that

\displaystyle\prod_{A_{imm}(\psi^{+})\cup A_{imm}(\psi^{-})}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}\,}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a}){\widehat{V_{y_{a+1}}}}(q_{a+1})

\displaystyle\times{\mathbf{E}\,}\Big[\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in K^{+}\\
\psi^{+}(a)\not=c_{imm}\end{subarray}}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}^{(\psi^{+}(a))}}}(q_{a}))\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in K^{-}\\
\psi^{-}(a)\not=c_{imm}\end{subarray}}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}^{(\psi^{-}(a))}}}(q_{a}))\Big]

\displaystyle=\prod_{S\in P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})}{\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{a\in S}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a}).

Then, defining the colored operator
O^{\Psi}_{\Gamma}
associated to a set
of colorings

O^{\Psi}_{\Gamma}=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi}O^{\psi}_{\Gamma},

we have

{\mathbf{E}\,}(O^{\Psi^{+}}_{\Gamma^{+}})^{*}AO^{\Psi^{-}}_{\Gamma^{-}}=\sum_{P\in{\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{+},\Psi^{-})}{\mathbf{E}\,}_{P}(O_{\Gamma^{+}})^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{-}}.

In Section
9

we will construct sets of
of colorings
\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}})
and
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})

associated to a skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}
so that

{\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}}),\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}}))={\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{F}}
.
In particular, this implies that for
N^{4}
-complete extended paths
\bm{\Gamma}

with skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}
, we have the formula

(8.3)

{\mathbf{E}\,}O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{-}}={\mathbf{E}\,}(O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}})})^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{-}}^{\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})}.

8.2.
The product structure of
O_{\Gamma}

We observe that the path operator
O_{\Gamma}
has the product structure

O_{\Gamma}=O_{\Gamma,1}O_{\Gamma,2}\cdots O_{\Gamma,N}

where

O_{\Gamma,\ell}=\mathinner{|{\xi_{2\ell-2}}\rangle}\mathinner{\langle{\xi_{2\ell-2}|O_{\omega_{\ell}}|\xi_{2\ell-1}}\rangle}\mathinner{\langle{\xi_{2\ell-1}}|}.

Given an index
\ell
, we write
\Gamma[\ell]
for the
tuple of variables
(\xi_{2\ell-2},O_{\omega_{\ell}},\xi_{2\ell-1})
.

The colored path operators have a similar structure

O_{\Gamma}^{\psi}=O_{\Gamma,1}^{\psi_{1}}\cdots O_{\Gamma,N}^{\psi_{N}}

where

O_{\Gamma[\ell]}^{\psi_{\ell}}=\mathinner{|{\xi_{2\ell-2}}\rangle}\mathinner{\langle{\xi_{2\ell-2}|O_{\omega_{\ell}}^{\psi_{\ell}}|\xi_{2\ell-1}}\rangle}\mathinner{\langle{\xi_{2\ell-1}}|}.

We introduce another notation to write these products using the tensor product space

{\mathcal{H}}_{N}:=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{N}L^{2}({\mathbf{R}}^{d}).

For each
\ell\in[N]
we define the map

\iota_{\ell}:{\mathcal{B}}(L^{2}({\mathbf{R}}^{d}))\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{N})

which sends an operator
A
to an operator
\iota_{\ell}(A)
acting locally at the
\ell
-th slot.
That is, we write

\iota_{\ell}(A):=\Id\otimes\cdots\otimes\Id\otimes A\otimes\Id\otimes\cdots\otimes\Id

where the
A
appears in the
\ell
-th tensor product slot. We also define the map

\Mult:{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{N})\to{\mathcal{B}}(L^{2}({\mathbf{R}}^{d}))

which is defined on simple tensors by the formula

\Mult(A_{1}\otimes A_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{N})=A_{1}A_{2}\cdots A_{N},

and which extends by linearity to the space
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{N})
.

Using this notation we can express the product structure
of
O_{\Gamma}
for example by the identity

O_{\Gamma}=\Mult(\prod_{\ell=1}^{N}\iota_{\ell}(O_{\Gamma,\ell})),

with no need to worry about the ordering of the product since
\iota_{\ell}(A)
commutes with

\iota_{\ell^{\prime}}(B)
when
\ell\not=\ell^{\prime}
.

8.3.
Product structure for coloring sets

In this section we describe two kinds of product structure that can be used to simplify the operator

O^{\Psi}_{\Gamma}
.

Let
Q\in{\mathcal{P}}([N])
be a partition of the segment index set
[N]
. Let
(\psi_{q})_{q\in Q}
be a
tuple of local coloring functions
\psi_{q}:K_{q}\to{\mathcal{C}}
with domains
K_{q}\subset\ell^{-1}(q)
. Such
a tuple defines a global coloring function
\bigoplus_{q}\psi_{q}:K\to{\mathcal{C}}
with
K=\bigcup K_{q}
, defined so that

(\bigoplus_{q}\psi_{q})|_{K_{q^{\prime}}}=\psi_{q^{\prime}}
. Given sets of local colorings
\Psi_{q}
, we define the product

\prod_{q\in Q}\Psi_{q}:=\{\bigoplus_{q\in Q}\psi_{q}\mid\psi_{q}\in\Psi_{q}\}.

We say moreover that the tuple of coloring function sets
\{\Psi_{q}\}_{q\in Q}

splits

if there is a partition of the external collision colors
{\mathcal{C}}_{ext}=\bigcup_{q\in Q}{\mathcal{C}}_{q}

into disjoint sets
{\mathcal{C}}_{q}
such that, for every
\psi_{q}\in\Psi_{q}
,
\Range(\psi_{q})\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{q}\cup{\mathcal{C}}_{imm}
.

Given a local coloring function
\psi_{q}\in\Psi_{q}
, we define the local path operator
O^{\psi_{q}}_{\Gamma}
by

O^{\psi_{q}}_{\Gamma}=\prod_{\ell\in q}\iota_{q}(O^{\psi_{\ell}}_{\Gamma_{\ell}}).

Then we set
O^{\Psi_{q}}_{\Gamma}=\sum_{\psi_{q}\in\Psi_{q}}O^{\psi_{q}}_{\Gamma}
.

The following observation connects the product structure of a coloring set
\Psi
to the tensor product
structure of the operators
O^{\Psi}_{\Gamma}
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
Q\in{\mathcal{P}}([N])
be a partition of
[N]
, and let
Let
\Psi=\prod_{q\in Q}\Psi_{q}
be a set of coloring functions that is a product of sets of local coloring
functions
\Psi_{q}
. Then the operator
O^{\Psi}_{\Gamma}
has the product structure

O^{\Psi}_{\Gamma}=\Mult\big(\prod_{q\in Q}O^{\Psi_{q}}_{\Gamma}\big).

If moreover
\Psi
splits with respect to
Q
, so that
\Range(\Psi_{q})\cap\Range(\Psi_{q^{\prime}})\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{imm}

for
q\not=q^{\prime}
, then the operators
O^{\Psi_{q}}_{\Gamma}
are independent.

The independence of the operators
O^{\Psi_{q}}_{\Gamma}
is useful because it allows us to split up the expectation of
expressions involving
O^{\Psi}_{\Gamma}
. In particular we will be computing
{\mathbf{E}\,}(O^{\Psi}_{\Gamma^{+}})^{*}O^{\Psi}_{\Gamma^{-}}
.
To write an expression for this operator we work on the squared Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{N}

and introduce the squared multiplication map

\Mult^{\pm}(A_{1}^{+}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{N}^{+}\otimes A_{1}^{-}\otimes\cdots A_{N}^{-}):=(A_{N}^{+})^{*}(A_{N-1}^{+})^{*}\cdots(A_{1}^{+})^{*}A_{1}^{-}A_{2}^{-}\cdots A_{N}^{-}.

With this definition, we have for any paths
\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}
and any coloring functions
\psi^{+},\psi^{-}

the identity

(O^{\psi^{+}}_{\Gamma^{+}})^{*}O^{\psi^{-}}_{\Gamma^{-}}=\Mult^{\pm}\big(\prod_{\ell\in[N]}\iota_{\ell}(O^{\psi^{+}_{\ell}}_{\Gamma^{+}_{\ell}})\otimes\iota_{\ell}(O^{\psi^{-}_{\ell}}_{\Gamma^{-}_{\ell}})\big).

Then, if
\Psi=\prod_{q\in Q}\Psi_{q}
and
\Psi_{q}
split
{\mathcal{C}}_{ext}
, then

{\mathbf{E}\,}(O^{\Psi}_{\Gamma^{+}})^{*}O^{\Psi}_{\Gamma^{-}}=\Mult^{\pm}\big(\prod_{q\in Q}{\mathbf{E}\,}O^{\Psi_{q}}_{\Gamma^{+}}\otimes O^{\Psi_{q}}_{\Gamma^{-}}\big).

9.
Constructing partitions from colorings

In this section we provide the construction for the coloring set
\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}})
and
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})

such that
{\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}}),\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}}))={\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
for a skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}
.
We then show how to
\Psi^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}})
into components that have a product structure and which split.
This construction will be used to simplify the calculation of the diagrammatic expansion.

9.1.
Coloring a ladder

The building blocks of the partitions in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{F}

are renormalized ladders and anti-ladders. In this section
we demonstrate how to construct these partitions from colorings. We will construct the set of colorings
by first assigning to each renormalized ladder a canonical coloring.

To define the colorings, we first investigate in some more detail the structure of a ladder partition.
Let
A
and
B
be disjoint ordered sets, and let
P\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{rl}(A\sqcup B)
be a renormalized
ladder partition. The cells in
P
are all pairs, and come two distinct types. The first type is a
“rung”. A rung is a pair
(a,b)\in A\times B
. Let
\Rung(P)\subset P
be the set of rungs in
P
.
The second type is an immediate recollision of the form
(a,a+_{A}1)
or
(b,b+_{B}1)
. Let

\Imm(P)
be the set of immediate recollisions.

We place an ordering on the set of rungs
in which
(a,b)\leq(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
if
a\leq a^{\prime}
.
There is a unique order-preserving bijection

\varphi_{\Rung(P)}:\Rung(P)\to[|\Rung(P)|]
which enumerates the runs.

We are ready to define the canonical coloring of
A\sqcup B
associated to the renormalized ladder
P
.
The color palette
{\mathcal{C}}
is given by

{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{C}}_{ext}\cup\{c_{imm}\}=\{{\mlq\mlq\ext\mrq\mrq}\}\times{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{{\mlq\mlq\Imrec\mrq\mrq}\}.

For
x\in A\cup B
, we define the canonical coloring

(9.1)

\psi_{P}(x)=\begin{cases}({\mlq\mlq\ext\mrq\mrq},\varphi_{\Rung(P)}(a,b)),&x\in(a,b)\in\Rung(P)\\
{\mlq\mlq\Imrec\mrq\mrq}&x\in\supp\Imm(P).\end{cases}

We note that this construction works without modification if
P
is a renormalized anti-ladder partition.
By construction, the coloring function
\psi_{P}
recovers the ladder partition
P

in the following way.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
P\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{rl}(A\sqcup B)
be a renormalized (anti-)ladder on
A\sqcup B
, and let
\psi_{P}
be
the coloring defined in (
9.1
). Let
\psi_{A}=(\psi_{P})|_{A}
and
\psi_{B}=(\psi_{P})|_{B}
. Then

P=P(\psi_{A},\psi_{B})
, where
P(\psi_{A},\psi_{B})
is defined by Definition
8.1
.

We define
\Psi^{+}_{rl}(A)
to be the set of all coloring functions of the form
(\psi_{P})|_{A}

for some
P\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{rl}(A,B)
where
B
is any finite set. That is,

\Psi^{+}_{rl}(A)=\{(\psi_{P})|_{A}\mid P\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{rl}(A,B)\text{ for some finite set }B\}.

We likewise define
\Psi^{-}_{rl}(A)
to be the half-coloring functions generated from
anti-ladders
.
If
P
is an antiladder on
A\sqcup B
, then in fact
(\psi_{P})|_{A}\in\Psi^{+}_{rl}(A)
(since the numbering
of the rungs is still increasing on
A
), whereas
(\psi_{P})|_{B}
has decreasing rung indices. Therefore we
define

\Psi^{-}_{rl}(A)=\{(\psi_{P})|_{A}\mid P\in{\mathcal{Q}}^{-}_{rl}(B,A)\text{ for some finite set }B\}.

We will need to slightly modify the definitions of
\Psi^{\pm}_{rl}(A)
to allow for labels other
than
{\mlq\mlq\ext\mrq\mrq}
in the color palette
{\mathcal{C}}
. Let
\lambda_{ext}
be such a label,
and define the relabelled color palette

{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda_{ext}):=\{\lambda_{ext}\}\times{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{{\mlq\mlq\Imrec\mrq\mrq}\}.

Then we set
\Psi^{\pm}_{rl}(A;\lambda_{ext})
to be the analogues of
\Psi^{\pm}_{rl}(A)

on the color palette
{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda_{ext})
.

As a consequence of Lemma
9.1
we see that the coloring
sets
\Psi^{\pm}_{rl}(A)
can be used to reconstruct the generalized ladder partitions.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
A
and
B
be finite sets. Then the following hold:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{rl}(A,B)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{+}_{rl}(A),\Psi^{+}_{rl}(B))={\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{-}_{rl}(A),\Psi^{-}_{rl}(B))

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}^{-}_{rl}(A,B)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{+}_{rl}(A),\Psi^{-}_{rl}(B)).

Now we define a partition of the ladder colorings according to the number of rungs.
Set
\Psi^{\pm,h}_{rl}(A)\subset\Psi^{\pm}_{rl}(A)
to be the colorings with exactly
h
rungs,
that is,

\Psi^{\pm,h}_{rl}(A)=\{\psi\in\Psi^{\pm}_{rl}(A)\mid\psi^{-1}(({\mlq\mlq ext\mrq\mrq},h))\not=\varnothing\text{ and }\psi^{-1}(({\mlq\mlq ext\mrq\mrq},h+1))=\varnothing\}.

Note that if
h\not=h^{\prime}
and
\psi\in\Psi^{\pm,h}_{rl}(A)
and

\psi^{\prime}\in\Psi^{\pm,h}_{rl}(B)
, then
P(\psi,\psi^{\prime})
has a singleton set.

9.2.
Coloring a skeleton

Now we use the construction from the previous section to define colorings
\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}};K^{+})

and
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};K^{-})
that assemble the partitions of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
.
Given a skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}
, define the color palette

{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{F}})=P_{\mathcal{F}}\,\,\cup\,{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})^{2}\times{\mathbb{N}}\,\cup\,\{{\mlq\mlq\Imrec\mrq\mrq}\}

Roughly speaking, the first set of colors
P_{{\mathcal{F}}}
will be used to color the collisions
in
\supp{\mathcal{F}}
, while the second set of colors
{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})^{2}\times{\mathbb{N}}
are used to color
the rungs of ladders, and
{\mlq\mlq\Imrec\mrq\mrq}
is used for immediate recollisions.

Definition 9.3

(Colorings of
K^{+}
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a skeleton and
K^{+}
be a collision set containing
\supp{\mathcal{F}}\cap K^{+}_{all}
.
The coloring collection
\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}};K^{+})
is the collection of functions

\psi:K^{+}\to{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{F}})
such that the following holds for some
partial matching
M
of
{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})
:

•

For
a\in\supp{\mathcal{F}}
,
\psi(a)=S\in P_{{\mathcal{F}}}
is the cell
S
containing the
index
a
.

•

If
I\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})
with
\sign(I)=1
is an abstract interval
that is not matched by
M
,
I\not\in\supp M
, then
\psi^{+}(a)={\mlq\mlq\Imrec\mrq\mrq}
for every

a\in I_{K}
.

•

If
(I,I^{\prime})\in M
is a pair in
M
and
\sign(I)=+1
, then

\psi^{+}|_{I_{K^{+}}}\in\Psi^{+}(I_{K^{+}};\{I,I^{\prime}\})
.

•

For every
\ell\in[N]
, the number of collisions
a
satisfying
\ell(a)=\ell

and
\psi(a)={\mlq\mlq\Imrec\mrq\mrq}
is even.

We then write
\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}})=\bigcup_{K^{+}}\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}};K^{+})
, where the union is
over all collision sets
K^{+}
.

The colorings
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};K^{-})
are defined in a very similar way, but with the possibility
that an interval is colored as an anti-ladder.

Definition 9.4

(Colorings of
K^{-}
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a skeleton and
K^{-}
be a collision set containing
\supp{\mathcal{F}}\cap K^{-}_{all}
.
The coloring collection
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};K^{-})
is the collection of functions

\psi:K^{-}\to{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{F}})
such that the following holds for some
partial matching
M
of
{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})
and some
orientation function
\aleph:M\to\{\pm\}

•

For
a\in\supp{\mathcal{F}}
,
\psi(a)=S\in P_{{\mathcal{F}}}
is the cell
S
containing the
index
a
.

•

If
I\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})
with
\sign(I)=1
is an abstract interval
that is not matched by
M
,
I\not\in\supp M
, then
\psi^{+}(a)={\mlq\mlq\Imrec\mrq\mrq}
for every

a\in I_{K}
.

•

If
(I,I^{\prime})\in M
is a pair in
M
and
\sign(I^{\prime})=-1
, then

\psi^{+}|_{I_{K^{+}}}\in\Psi^{\aleph(I,I^{\prime})}(I_{K^{-}};I,(I,I^{\prime}))
.

•

For every
\ell\in[N]
, the number of collisions
a
satisfying
\ell(a)=\ell

and
\psi(a)={\mlq\mlq\Imrec\mrq\mrq}
is even.

We then write
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})=\bigcup_{K^{-}}\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};K^{-})
, where the union is
over all collision sets
K^{-}
.

The following lemma shows that the colorings
\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}})
and
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})
reconstruct
the family of partitions
{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Lemma 9.5
.

The coloring sets
\Psi^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}})
assemble
{\mathcal{Q}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
in the sense that

{\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}}),\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}}))={\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{F}}.

In the remainder of the section we will partition
\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}})
and
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})
into
pieces that have a product structure and which split in the way described in Section
8.3
.

9.3.
Partitioning
\Psi^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}})
into sets with product structure

There are two reasons that
\Psi^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}})
cannot not have a product structure with respect
to a partition
Q\in{\mathcal{P}}([N])
. The first is that there are global correlations across segment
intervals coming from the matching
M
. The second is that the rung counts within a ladder communicate
across segments. We will partition
\Psi^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}})
into sets with fixed matchings
M
and fixed rung
counts so that within certain intervals of
[N]
we recover a product structure. The partition we
choose also has some extra information that will be useful in the diarammatic estimates.

We start by partitioning the
\Psi^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}})
according to the matching
M
.
We define the matching
M
associated to a coloring
\psi
to be the set of all
pairs of abstract intervals
(I,I^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})
which share at least one rung:

M_{\psi}:=\{(I,I^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})\mid\psi^{-1}((\{I,I^{\prime}\},1))\not=\varnothing\}.

Given
(I,I^{\prime})\in M_{\psi}
we then define the orientation

\aleph_{\psi}(I,I^{\prime}):=\begin{cases}-,\psi^{-1}((\{I,I^{\prime}\}),2)\cap K^{+}<\psi^{-1}((\{I,I^{\prime}\}),1)\cap K^{+}\\
+,\text{ else}.\end{cases}

That is,
\aleph_{\psi}(I,I^{\prime})=-1
if
(I,I^{\prime})
has at least two rungs and they are in reverse order, and is

+1
otherwise.

We partition
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})
according to the values of
M
and
\psi
,

\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};M,\aleph):=\{\psi\in\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})\mid M_{\psi}=M,\aleph_{\psi}=\aleph\}.

This yields a partition of
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})
into sets
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};M,\aleph)
into sets which
share the same global properties.

We further subdivide
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};M,\aleph)
according to the rung counts at specified collisions.
Given a coloring
\psi
we define the rung count function
{\overline{\psi}}
by

{\overline{\psi}}(a)=\begin{cases}j,&\psi(a)=(\{I,I^{\prime}\},j)\text{ for some }(I,I^{\prime})\in M\\
\varnothing,&\text{ else.}\end{cases}

We also define the segment rung count function
m_{\psi}(a)
to be the number of rungs within the segment
(and interval ) containing
a
,

m_{\psi}(a):=\#\{b\in I_{K}\cup I^{\prime}_{K}\mid\psi(b)=(\{I,I^{\prime}\},j)\text{ and }\ell(b)=\ell(a)\}.

Finally we define the neighbors
\triangleright_{\psi}(a)
and
\triangleleft_{\psi}(a)

of an index
a
. These are the next rung after the index
a
and the previous rung before index
a
,
respectively,

\displaystyle\triangleright_{\psi}(a)

\displaystyle=\min\{a^{\prime}\mid a^{\prime}>a\text{ and }\psi(a^{\prime})\in M\times{\mathbb{N}}\}

\displaystyle\triangleleft_{\psi}(a)

\displaystyle=\max\{a^{\prime}\mid a^{\prime}<a\text{ and }\psi(a^{\prime})\in M\times{\mathbb{N}}\}.

In the above definitions we use the convention
\min\varnothing=\infty
and
\max\varnothing=0
,
so that
\triangleright_{\psi}(a)=\infty
if there are no rungs after
a
, and
\triangleleft_{\psi}=0
if there are
no rungs preceding
a
.

Given an index
a
, we then define the neighborhood tuple

\Nbd_{\psi}(a):=({\overline{\psi}}(a),\triangleright_{\psi}(a),{\overline{\psi}}(\triangleright_{\psi}(a)),m_{\psi}(\triangleright_{\psi}(a)),\triangleleft_{\psi}(a),{\overline{\psi}}(\triangleleft_{\psi}(a)),m_{\psi}(\triangleleft_{\psi}(a)))

Then given a set
S
we define
\Nbd_{\psi}(S)=(\Nbd_{\psi}(a))_{a\in S}
.
Given a set
S
and a neighborhood tuple
\Nbd(S)
of a set we define the coloring sets

\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};M,\aleph,\Nbd(S)):=\{\psi\in\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};M,\aleph)\mid\Nbd_{\psi}(S)=\Nbd(S)\}.

These coloring sets clearly partition
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})
, and we will next show that they
have a product structure. We call the tuples
({\mathcal{F}},M,\aleph,\Nbd(S))

scaffolds
.
1
1

1

A scaffold is a structure that can support many ladders and sticks.

The specific choice we make for the set
S
is to take
S=\supp^{-}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
for
some skeleton
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\supset{\mathcal{F}}
. We write

{\mathcal{S}}^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
for the set of scaffolds of the form

\Scaff=({\mathcal{F}},M,\aleph,\Nbd(\supp^{\pm}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}))
. Then given any
skeletons
{\mathcal{F}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\supset{\mathcal{F}}
, we have the partition

\Psi^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}})=\bigcup_{{\mathcal{S}}^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\Psi^{\pm}(\Scaff).

We observe that there are not
too many
scaffolds associated to a pair of skeletons
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
,

(9.2)

\#{\mathcal{S}}^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\leq N^{C\|{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\|}.

We will show in the next section that
\Psi^{\pm}(\Scaff)
has a product structure as described in
Section
8.3
.

9.3.1.
Product structure of
\Psi^{-}(\Scaff)

For this section we fix a scaffold
\Scaff=({\mathcal{F}};M,\aleph,\Nbd(S))
and demonstrate
that
\Psi^{\pm}(\Scaff)
has a product structure. Again we focus for concreteness on the set

\Psi^{-}(\Scaff)
which is slightly more complicated than
\Psi^{+}(\Scaff)
.
We define the
base
of the scaffold
\Scaff
to be the set
S=\supp^{-}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
along with the neighboring rungs specified
by
\Nbd(S)
,

\Base(\Scaff)=S\cup\{\triangleright(a)\}_{a\in S}\cup\{\triangleleft(a)\}_{a\in S}.

We will write simply
\Base
instead of
\Base(\Scaff)
in this section, as we have fixed for now the scaffold

\Scaff
.
Then define

{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)
to be the abstract interval collection with endpoints

\Base
. Since
S\subset\supp{\mathcal{F}}
, for each
J={]{a,b}[}\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)

there exists a unique
I={]{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}[}\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})
such that

a^{\prime}\leq a
and
b\leq b^{\prime}
. In particular, for any collision set
K
and

J\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)
,
J_{K}\subset I_{K}
for some
I\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})

We say that
J\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)
is

ladder-like
if
J={]{a,b}[}
with
a,b\not\in S
. Otherwise we say that

J\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)
is a
stick
if
J={]{a,b}[}
and
a\in S
or
b\in S
.
Note that if
J
is ladder-like then
J={]{\triangleright(s),\triangleleft(s^{\prime})}[}
for some
s<s^{\prime}\in S

(since every element in
\Base\setminus S
is of the form
\triangleright(s)
or
\triangleleft(s)
for some
s\in S
).

We use the intervals
{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)
to partition the set
[N]
as follows.
To each
J={]{a,b}[}\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)
we define the set

\ell(J):={]{\ell(a),\ell(b)}[}=[\ell(a)+1,\ell(b)-1]\subset[N].

Note that
\ell(J)
may be empty if
\ell(b)\leq\ell(a)+1
.

The intervals
\ell(J)
can be used to form the partition
Q\in{\mathcal{P}}([N])

Q(\Base)=\{\ell(\Base)\}\cup\{\ell(J)\}_{J\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)}.

Given a realization of a collision index set
K
, we define the partition

\ell^{-1}Q(\Base)\in{\mathcal{P}}(K)
by

\ell^{-1}Q(\Base):=\{K\cap\ell^{-1}(\Base),\{K\cap\ell^{-1}(\ell(J))\}_{J\in\pi({\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base))}\}

The first set in the partition is called the “junk” set
\Junk(\Base,K)
, and the latter
sets are written

J^{\circ}_{K}
, so

J^{\circ}_{K}=\ell^{-1}\ell(J)\cap K=\{a\in K\mid\ell(a)\in\ell(K),a\in J_{K}\}.

Now we are ready to show that the sets
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};M,\aleph,\Nbd(S))
have a product structure
with respect to the partition
Q
. To write the product structure of

\Psi^{\pm}(\Scaff)
, define for each
q\in Q(\Base)
the set of coloring functions

(9.3)

\Psi^{\pm}_{q}(\Scaff):=\{\psi|_{\ell^{-1}(q)}\mid\psi\in\Psi^{\pm}(\Scaff)\}.

That is,
\Psi^{\pm}_{q}
consists of the restrictions of the colorings
\psi
in
\Psi^{\pm}
to collisions
belonging to the interval
q
.

The main result is the following.

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
\Scaff\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
for a pair of skeletons

{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
.
Then, with
\Psi_{q}^{-}(\Scaff)
defined by (
9.3
),
the coloring set

\Psi^{-}(\Scaff)
has the product structure

(9.4)

\Psi^{-}(\Scaff)=\prod_{q\in Q(\Base)}\Psi_{q}^{-}(\Scaff).

Moreover, letting
{\mathcal{C}}_{q}=\bigcup_{\psi_{q}\in\Psi^{-}_{q}}\Range(\psi_{q})
, we have
for
q\not=q^{\prime}
the inclusion
{\mathcal{C}}_{q}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{q^{\prime}}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{imm}
.

To prove Lemma
9.6
we first
make a simple observation concerning the structure of colorings in

\Psi^{-}(\Scaff)
.

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
J\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)
be an abstract interval, and let

\psi\in\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};M,\aleph,\Nbd(S))
be a coloring on collision set
K
.
Let
I\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})
be the unique interval satisfying
J_{K}\subset I_{K}
.
Then one of the following holds:

•

The interval
J
is a stick, and in this case
\psi(J^{\circ}_{K})\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{imm}
,
or in other words
\psi|_{J^{\circ}_{K}}\in\Psi^{+,0}_{rl}(J^{\circ}_{K})
.

•

The interval
J
is ladder-like, so that
\{I,I^{\prime}\}\in M
for some
I^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}({\mathcal{F}})

and
J=(\triangleright(s),\triangleleft(s^{\prime}))
for some
(s,s^{\prime})\in S
. Now if
\aleph(\{I,I^{\prime}\})=+1
then

(9.5)

\psi(J^{\circ}_{K})\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{imm}=\{\{I,I^{\prime}\}\}\times\{{\overline{\psi}}(\triangleright(s))+m(\triangleright(s))\leq j\leq{\overline{\psi}}(\triangleleft(s^{\prime}))-m(\triangleleft(s^{\prime}))\}.

Otherwise if
\aleph(\{I,I^{\prime}\})=-1
then

\psi(J^{\circ}_{K})\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{imm}=\{\{I,I^{\prime}\}\}\times\{{\overline{\psi}}(\triangleleft(s^{\prime}))+m(\triangleleft(s^{\prime}))\leq j\leq{\overline{\psi}}(\triangleright(s))-m(\triangleright(s))\}.

Proof.

If
J
is a stick, then
J=(s,\triangleright(s))
or
J=(\triangleleft(s),s)
for some
s\in S
. In particular,
by the definition of the functions
\triangleright
and
\triangleleft
we have
\psi(J_{K})\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{imm}
.
Since
J^{\circ}_{K}\subset J_{K}
this proves the first claim.

We prove the second claim when
\aleph=+1
, as the case
\aleph=-1
is similar. In this
case,
J=(\triangleright(s),\triangleleft(s^{\prime}))
, so we have the following identity for the rung count function

{\overline{\psi}}
on the interval
J_{K}
:

{\overline{\psi}}(J_{K})=]{\overline{\psi}}(\triangleright(s)),{\overline{\psi}}(\triangleleft(s))[.

The claim then follows from observing for example that

{\overline{\psi}}(a)-{\overline{\psi}}\triangleright(s)\in[0,m(\triangleright(s)]
implies
\ell(a)=\ell(\triangleright(s))\not\in\ell(J)
,
whereas
{\overline{\psi(a)}}-{\overline{\psi}}(\triangleright(s))>m(\triangleright(s)
implies
\ell(a)>\ell(\triangleright(s))
.
∎

We are now ready to prove Lemma
9.6
.

Proof of Lemma
9.6
.

To prove the product structure of
\Psi^{-}(\Scaff)
it suffices to show
the following: for any
\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in\Psi^{-}(\Scaff)
and any
J\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)
,
the coloring
\varphi
defined by
\varphi(a)=\psi(a)
for
a\in J^{\circ}_{K}
and
\varphi(a)=\psi^{\prime}(a)

otherwise is also a valid coloring in
\Psi^{-}(\Scaff)
. This is straightforward, and the
key idea is that the number of rungs in any
J^{\circ}_{K}
is fixed, so swapping the values of
\psi
for
\psi^{\prime}

on
J^{\circ}_{K}
does not affect
\Nbd_{\psi}(S)
.

On the other hand, the fact that
\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}};M,\aleph,\Nbd(S))
splits according to the partition
Q(\Base)

follows from Lemma
9.7
and observing that the sets described
in (
9.5
) are disjoint.
∎

The final result we need in this section is a characterization of the partition
sets
{\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{-}_{q}(\Scaff))
, which is a simple consequence
of Lemma
9.7
.

Lemma 9.8
.

Let
(M,\aleph,\Nbd)
as above, and let
J\in{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base(\Nbd))
be an abstract
interval, and let
q=\ell(J)\in Q(\Base)
. If
J
is a stick interval,
then the partition collection
{\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{-}_{q}(\Scaff))

consists of simple partitions. On the other hand if
J=]\triangleright(s),\triangleleft(s^{\prime})[
is a ladder
interval, then
{\mathcal{Q}}(\Psi^{-}_{q}(\Scaff))
consists of generalized ladder partitions
with exactly

{\overline{\psi}}(\triangleleft(s^{\prime}))-{\overline{\psi}}(\triangleright(s))+1-m(\triangleleft(s^{\prime}))-m(\triangleright(s))

rungs.

10.
The diagrammatic expansion

In this section we start to derive our diagrammatic decomposition of
the evolution channel
{\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}}_{N\tau}[A]
.
We start with the expression

\displaystyle{\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}}_{N\tau}[A]=\int_{\Omega_{ext}^{N}\times\Omega_{ext}^{N}}\Xi(\bm{\Gamma}){\mathbf{1}}(\Gamma^{+}\sim\Gamma^{-}){\mathbf{E}\,}O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{-}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{+}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{-},

First we decompose the integral according to the skeleton of the path
\bm{\Gamma}
.
We first rewrite the expectation using the skeleton operators
using (
8.3
), then we apply Lemma
7.5

to rewrite the indicator function for a skeleton:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{N\tau}[A]

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathcal{F}}}\iint\Xi(\bm{\Gamma}){\mathbf{1}}(\Gamma^{+}\sim\Gamma^{-}){\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}(\bm{\Gamma}){\mathbf{E}\,}(O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}})})^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{-}}^{\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathcal{F}}\leq{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}\iint\Xi(\bm{\Gamma})\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\bm{\Gamma}){\mathbf{E}\,}(O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}})})^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{-}}^{\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}.

We define
{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}
to be the superoperator appearing on the right hand side,

{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}[A]:=\iint\Xi(\bm{\Gamma}){\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}(\bm{\Gamma}){\mathbf{E}\,}(O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{\Psi^{+}({\mathcal{F}})})^{*}AO_{\Gamma^{-}}^{\Psi^{-}({\mathcal{F}})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}.

Then we rewrite our decomposition of the evolution channel as

(10.1)

{\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}}_{N\tau}[A]=\sum_{{\mathcal{F}}\leq{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F^{\prime}}}}[A].

The main term comes from the pair of empty skeletons,
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}=\bm{0}
.
In this case the expectation only includes ladder partitions, and moreover

\chi_{\bm{0},\bm{0}}=1
, so the main term is in fact the ladder superoperator

(10.2)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{E}}_{\bm{0},\bm{0}}[A]&=\iint\Xi(\bm{\Gamma}){\mathbf{E}\,}(O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{lad})^{*}A(O_{\Gamma^{-}}^{lad})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}\\
&={\mathcal{L}}_{N\tau}[A].\end{split}

Now we deal with the terms with
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\not=\bm{0}
. For any tuple

Y:J^{\pm}_{clust}\to{\mathbf{R}}^{d}
and any scaffold
\Scaff\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
we
define the operator

(10.3)

O_{\pm,\Scaff,Y}:=\int\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}^{\pm,Y}(\Gamma)\Xi(\Gamma)O_{\Gamma}^{\Psi^{\pm}(\Scaff)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\Gamma

usin the function
G_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}^{\pm,Y}
defined in (
7.3
).
Then using (
7.4
) and the decomposition

\Psi^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}})=\bigcup_{\Scaff\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\Psi(\Scaff),

we have the identity

(10.4)

{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}[A]=\sum_{\Scaff^{+}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{+}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\sum_{\Scaff^{-}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{-}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\iint\chi^{clust}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(Y^{+},Y^{-}){\mathbf{E}\,}O_{+,\Scaff^{+},Y^{+}}^{*}AO_{-,\Scaff^{-},Y^{-}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Y^{+}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Y^{-},

where the integral is over tuples
Y^{+}:J^{+}_{clust}\to{\mathbf{R}}^{d}

and
Y^{-}:J^{-}_{clust}\to{\mathbf{R}}^{d}
.

Now we use the following version of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, which
is that for any measure
\mu
on a measure space
\Omega
, if

X,Y:\Omega\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})

are operator-valued functions on a Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}
then

\big\|\int X(\theta)^{*}AY(\theta)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\theta)\big\|_{op}\leq\|A\|_{op}\big\|\int X(\theta)^{*}X(\theta)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\theta)\big\|_{op}^{1/2}\big\|\int Y(\theta)^{*}Y(\theta)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu(\theta)\big\|_{op}^{1/2}.

We use this inequality with the measure being the product of the counting measure on

{\mathcal{S}}^{+}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
, the counting measure on
{\mathcal{S}}^{-}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
,
the Lebesgue measure on the anchor points
Y
, and the random measure on the potentials

V^{(c)}
to obtain

(10.5)

\begin{split}\Big\|{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}[A]\Big\|_{op}\leq CN^{C\|{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\|}\|A\|_{op}&\max_{\Scaff^{+}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{+}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\Big\|\iint\chi^{clust}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(Y){\mathbf{E}\,}O_{+,\Scaff^{+},Y^{+}}^{*}O_{+,\Scaff^{+},Y^{+}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Y^{+}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Y^{-}\Big\|_{op}^{1/2}\\
&\times\max_{\Scaff^{-}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{-}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\Big\|\iint\chi^{clust}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(Y){\mathbf{E}\,}O_{-,\Scaff^{-},Y^{-}}^{*}O_{-,\Scaff^{-},Y^{-}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Y^{+}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Y^{-}\Big\|_{op}^{1/2}.\end{split}

One simplification we can make in the operators above is to integrate out half of the pair
(Y^{+},Y^{-})

using the localization afforded by
\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}^{clust}
,

(10.6)

\Big\|\iint\chi^{clust}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(Y){\mathbf{E}\,}O_{\pm,\Scaff,Y^{\pm}}^{*}O_{\pm,\Scaff,Y^{\pm}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Y^{+}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Y^{-}\Big\|_{op}\leq(CNr)^{|J^{\mp}_{clust}|}\Big\|\int{\mathbf{E}\,}O_{\pm,\Scaff,Y^{\pm}}^{*}O_{\pm,\Scaff,Y^{\pm}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}Y^{\pm}\Big\|_{op}.

We define
X_{\Scaff,\pm}
to be the operator appearing in the right hand side above,
which simplifies to

(10.7)

\begin{split}X_{\Scaff,\pm}&=\int\Xi(\bm{\Gamma})\chi^{\pm}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{+})\chi^{\pm}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{-})\prod_{a\in J^{\pm}_{clust}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\delta(y_{a}^{+}-y_{a}^{-}){\mathbf{E}\,}(O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{\Psi^{\pm}(\Scaff)})^{*}O_{\Gamma^{-}}^{\Psi^{\pm}(\Scaff)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}\end{split}

after interating out the delta functions.

In Section
11
we will prove the following estimate on
X_{\Scaff,\pm}
.

Proposition 10.1
.

There exists constants
C,c>0
such that the following holds:
Let
{\mathcal{F}}\subset{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
be a pair of skeletons with
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\not=\bm{0}
, and let

\Scaff\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\pm}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
be a scaffold. Then the operator
X_{\Scaff,+}
defined above
satisfies the operator norm bound

\|X_{\Scaff,\pm}\|_{op}\leq N^{C\|{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\|}{\varepsilon}^{c\|{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\|_{\pm}}r^{-|J^{\pm}_{clust}|d},

where

\|{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\|_{\pm}:=|\supp^{\pm}(F^{\prime}_{rec}\cup F^{\prime}_{tube}\cup F^{\prime}_{cone})|+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\in F_{clust}\\
|S\cap K^{\pm}_{all}|>1\end{subarray}}(|S|-1).

The proof of Proposition
10.1
uses geometric estimates on the volume of
paths that have the recollision, tube, and cone events described by
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
to obtain the
factor of
{\varepsilon}^{c\|{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\|}
. Using Proposition
10.1
we can show
that
{\mathcal{{\mathcal{E}}}}_{N\tau}
is close to the ladder superoperator.

Corollary 10.2
.

There exists
\kappa>0
such that if
N\leq{\varepsilon}^{-\kappa}
and for
any operator
A
with good support, the followin approximation holds:

\|{\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}}_{N\tau}[A]-{\mathcal{L}}_{N\tau}[A]\|_{op}\leq N^{C}{\varepsilon}^{c}.

Proof.

The conclusion follows from combining Proposition
10.1

with (
10.6
) and (
10.5
), and using the counting bound

\{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\mid\|{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\|\leq k\}\leq N^{Ck}

to complete the sum over
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\not=\varnothing
.
∎

10.1.
On the remainders
{\mathcal{R}}_{j}

.

To approximate the evolution
{\mathcal{E}}_{N\tau}
by the cutoff Duhamel series

{\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}}_{N\tau}
as in (
3.10
), we need
to prove the bound (
3.11
)

\max_{j\in[N]}\sup_{0\leq s\leq\tau}\|{\mathbf{E}\,}R_{j,s}^{*}R_{j,s}\|_{op}\leq{\varepsilon}^{50}.

We will work with the case
j=N
and
s=\tau
, which is on the one hand the “most difficult” case
in terms of the volume of integration, but is at the same time the case that is notationally easiest
to work with. Specializing to this case we recall the definition (
3.9
) of the operator

R_{N,\tau}
below

R_{N,\tau}=\int_{\Omega_{k_{max}}(\tau)\times{\mathbf{R}}^{2d}\times{\mathbf{R}}^{2d}}\mathinner{|{\eta}\rangle}\mathinner{\langle{\xi}|}U_{\tau,\sigma}^{N-1}\chi_{\sigma}(\omega,\xi,\eta)\mathinner{\langle{\eta|O_{\omega}|\xi}\rangle}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\omega\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta.

Expanding the operator
U_{\tau,\sigma}^{N-1}
as an integral over extended paths,

\displaystyle R_{N,\tau}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{k_{max}}(\tau)\times{\mathbf{R}}^{2d}\times{\mathbf{R}}^{2d}}\int_{\Omega_{\alpha,S}^{N-1}}\mathinner{|{\eta}\rangle}\mathinner{\langle{\xi}|}O_{\Gamma}\Xi(\Gamma)\chi_{\sigma}(\omega,\xi,\eta)\mathinner{\langle{\eta|O_{\omega}|\xi}\rangle}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\omega\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{\alpha,S,err}^{N}}O_{\Gamma}\Xi(\Gamma)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\Gamma,

where
\Omega_{\alpha,S,err}^{N}\subset\Omega_{\alpha,S}^{N}
is the subset of extended paths
having
k_{max}
collisions in the final segment.

Now we sketch the proof of the bound (
3.11
). By performing the same decomposition
as in the previous subsection
(in particular, following the arguments leading to (
10.6
) and (
10.5
))
with
A=\Id
, we reach a bound of the form

\|{\mathbf{E}\,}R_{N,\tau}^{*}R_{N\tau}\|_{op}\leq\sum_{{\mathcal{F}}\leq{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}N^{C\|{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\|}\sum_{\Scaff\in{\mathcal{S}}^{+}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\|X_{\Scaff,+,err}\|_{op},

where the only modification in the argument is to keep the condition that the final segment has
k_{max}

collisions, as in

(10.8)

\begin{split}X_{\Scaff,\pm,err}:=\int_{\Omega_{\alpha,S,err}^{N}\times\Omega_{\alpha,S,err}^{N}}\Xi(\bm{\Gamma})\chi^{\pm}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{+})\chi^{\pm}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{-})\prod_{a\in J^{\pm}_{clust}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\delta(y_{a}^{+}-y_{a}^{-}){\mathbf{E}\,}(O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{\Psi^{\pm}(\Scaff)})^{*}O_{\Gamma^{-}}^{\Psi^{\pm}(\Scaff)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}.\end{split}

The improvement that yields the factor of
{\varepsilon}^{50}
is that, in the proof of Proposition
10.1

we will frequently use
{\varepsilon}^{2}\tau\leq 1
and discard such factors. By simply keeping the
factor of
({\varepsilon}^{2}\tau)^{k_{max}}={\varepsilon}^{1000}
and not bounding it naively by
1
we easily obtain the
desired factor of
{\varepsilon}^{50}
.

11.
Bounding the diffusive diagram contributions

In this section we prove Proposition
10.1
,
which is a bound on the operator norm of
X_{\Scaff,\pm}
.
For concreteness we will work with
X_{\Scaff,+}
.

11.1.
Rewriting the operator
X_{\Scaff,+}

First we recall the definition of the operator
X_{\Scaff,+}
,

(11.1)

\begin{split}X_{\Scaff,+}=\int\Xi(\bm{\Gamma})\chi^{+}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{+})\chi^{+}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{-})\prod_{a\in J^{+}_{clust}({\mathcal{F^{\prime}}})}\delta(y_{a}^{+}-y_{a}^{-}){\mathbf{E}\,}(O_{\Gamma^{+}}^{\Psi^{+}(\Scaff)})^{*}O_{\Gamma^{-}}^{\Psi^{+}(\Scaff)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}.\end{split}

We observe that the functions
\chi^{+}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{\pm})

depend only on
\Gamma^{\pm}[\Base]
, where
\Base=\Base(\Scaff)
is as defined
in Section
9.3.1
.
One last observation we make before we exploit the tensor product structure of the operator

O_{\Gamma^{\pm}}^{\Psi^{+}(\Scaff)}
is that there are compatibility conditions on the phase
space indpoints of intervals in
Q(\Base)
coming from Lemma
6.19
.
Recall that
{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)
consists of intervals with endpoints in
\Base
. Write

{\mathcal{I}}^{abs}(\Base)={\mathcal{I}}^{stick}(\Base)\cup{\mathcal{I}}^{ladder}(\Base),

where
{\mathcal{I}}^{stick}(\Base)
consists of those intervals which have as an endpoint a collision in

\supp^{+}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
and
{\mathcal{I}}^{ladder}(\Base)
are those intervals of the form
]\triangleright(s),\triangleleft(s^{\prime})[

for
s,s^{\prime}\in\supp^{+}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
. Then let
Q^{stick}(\Base)=\ell(J)
for
J\in{\mathcal{I}}^{stick}(\Base)

and
Q^{ladder}(\Base)=\ell(J)
for
J\in{\mathcal{I}}^{ladder}(\Base)
be the intervals in
[N]

corresponding to these stick and ladder intervals.

These intervals impose some compatibility conditions on the phase space points
\xi_{\ell}
.
In particular, for sticks
]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in Q^{stick}(\Base)
the compatibility condition is simply

d_{r}(\xi^{\pm}_{2\ell_{2}-2},U_{k\tau}(\xi^{\pm}_{2\ell_{1}-1}))\leq N^{8}.

For ladders, the compatibility condition is that there exists
s\in[-\tau,\tau]
such that

d_{r}(\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{1}-2},U_{s}(\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{1}-2}))\leq N^{8}

and that

d_{r}(\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{2}-1},U_{s}(\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{2}-1}))\leq N^{8}.

We write
T\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\xi,&\eta\\
\xi^{\prime},&\eta^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\big)
for the indicator function on the
twinnin condition on ladders and
S_{k}(\xi,\eta)
for the “stick” condition.

Now we use the tensor product structure of the operator
O_{\Gamma}^{\Psi^{+}(\Scaff)}

given by applying Lemma
8.2
to the coloring set
\Psi^{+}(\Scaff)

(which has the required product structure owing to Lemma
9.6
).
This yields

\displaystyle X_{\Scaff,+}

\displaystyle=\Mult^{\pm}\Big[\big(\int\Xi(\bm{\Gamma}[\Base])])\chi^{+}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{+})\chi^{+}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{-})\prod_{a\in J^{+}_{clust}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\delta(y_{a}^{+}-y_{a}^{-})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\prod_{]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{ladder})(\Base)}T\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\xi_{2\ell_{1}-1}^{+},&\xi_{2\ell_{2}-2}^{+}\\
\xi_{2\ell_{1}-1}^{-},&\xi_{2\ell_{2}-2}^{-}\end{smallmatrix}\big)\prod_{]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{stick})(\Base)}\begin{smallmatrix}S_{k}(\xi_{2\ell_{1}-1}^{+},\xi_{2\ell_{2}-2}^{+})\\
S_{k}(\xi_{2\ell_{1}-1}^{-},\xi_{2\ell_{2}-2}^{-})\end{smallmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\mathbf{E}\,}(O_{\Gamma^{+}[\Base]}^{\Psi^{+}_{\Base}(\Scaff)})^{*}\otimes O_{\Gamma^{-}[\Base]}^{\Psi^{+}_{\Base}(\Scaff)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}[\Base]\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{J\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{abs})(\Base)}\big(\int\Xi(\bm{\Gamma}[J]){\mathbf{E}\,}O_{\Gamma^{+}[J]}^{\Psi^{+}_{J}(\Scaff)}\otimes O_{\Gamma^{-}[J]}^{\Psi^{+}_{J}(\Scaff)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}[I]\big)\Big].

At this point we use the fact that for intervals
J\in{\mathcal{I}}^{ladder}(\Base)
,
the coloring sets
\Psi^{+}_{J}(\Scaff)
are (up to a relabelling of the colors) equal
to
\Psi_{rl}^{+,h}
for some
h
specified by the scaffold. This is a consequence of
Lemma
9.7
. We therefore have the identity
that (for some
h=h(J)
determined by the scaffold),

L_{k,=h}\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\xi,&\eta\\
\xi^{\prime},&\eta^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\big)=\int_{\Omega_{\alpha,\sigma}^{k}}\Xi(\bm{\Gamma}){\mathbf{E}\,}\mathinner{\langle{\xi|O^{\Psi^{+}_{J}(\Scaff)}_{\Gamma^{+}}|\eta}\rangle}\mathinner{\langle{\eta^{\prime}|O^{\Psi^{+}_{J}(\Scaff)}_{\Gamma^{-}}|\xi^{\prime}}\rangle}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}.

This allows us to collapse the multiplication map
\Mult^{\pm}
and obtain

(11.2)

\begin{split}X_{\Scaff,+}&=\sum_{\psi^{+},\psi^{-}\in\Psi^{+}(\Scaff)}\int\mathinner{|{\xi_{2N-1}^{+}}\rangle}\mathinner{\langle{\xi_{2N-1}^{-}}|}\Xi(\bm{\Gamma}[\Base])\chi^{+}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{+}[\Base])\chi^{+}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{-}[\Base])\\
&\qquad\prod_{a\in J^{+}_{clust}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\delta(y_{a}^{+}-y_{a}^{-})\prod_{]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{ladder})(\Scaff)}T\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{1}-1},&\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{2}-2}\\
\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{1}-1},&\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{2}-2}\end{smallmatrix}\big)L_{\ell_{2}-\ell_{1}-1,=h}\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{1}-1},&\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{2}-2}\\
\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{1}-1},&\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{2}-2}\end{smallmatrix}\big)\\
&\qquad\prod_{]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{stick})(\Scaff)}S_{k}(\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{1}-1},\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{2}-2})S_{k}(\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{1}-1},\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{2}-2})\\
&\qquad\prod_{\ell\in\ell(\Base)}\langle{\xi^{+}_{2\ell-2}|p_{\ell,0,+}}\rangle\langle{p_{\ell,k_{\ell},+}|\xi^{+}_{2\ell-1}}\rangle\langle{\xi^{-}_{2\ell-2}|p_{\ell,0,-}}\rangle\langle{p_{\ell,k_{\ell},-}|\xi^{-}_{2\ell-1}}\rangle e^{i\varphi(\omega_{\ell}^{+})-i\varphi(\omega_{\ell}^{-})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\prod_{A_{imm}(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})}{\mathbf{E}\,}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}}}(q_{a}){\widehat{V_{y_{a+1}}}}(q_{a+1}){\mathbf{E}\,}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in K(\bm{\Gamma}_{D})\\
\psi(a)\not={\mlq\mlq\Imrec\mrq\mrq}\end{subarray}}{\widehat{V_{y_{a}}^{\psi(a)}}}(q_{a})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}\end{split}

Now we apply the Schur test to obtain a bound on the operator norm.
Because
X_{\Scaff,+}
is a self-adjoint operator, we only need to bound the
“row sum” norm.
We also use the bounds

|\langle{\xi|p}\rangle|\leq Cr^{d/2}\exp(-c(r|p-\xi_{p}|)^{0.999})

and Lemma
B.3
to bound the expectation. Then to deal with the terms
involving the ladder functions
L_{k}
,
we use the maximal ladder function

M_{k}(\xi,\eta):=\sup_{\xi^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}}T\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\xi,&\eta\\
\xi^{\prime},&\eta^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\big)|L_{k}\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\xi,&\eta\\
\xi^{\prime},&\eta^{\prime}\end{smallmatrix}\big)|.

finally we arrive at the estimate

(11.3)

\begin{split}\|X_{\Scaff,+}\|_{op}\leq&(CN)^{C\|{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\|}\sup_{\xi_{2N-1}^{+}}\sum_{\psi^{+},\psi^{-}\in\Psi^{+}_{\Base}(\Scaff)}\sum_{P^{\prime}\leq P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})}\int\chi^{+}_{{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{+}[\Base])\chi^{+}_{{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{-}[\Base])\\
&\prod_{a\in J^{+}_{clust}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\delta(y_{a}^{+}-y_{a}^{-})\prod_{]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{ladder}(D))}M_{\ell_{2}-\ell_{1}-1}(\xi_{2\ell_{2}-1}^{+},\xi_{2\ell_{2}-2}^{+})T\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{1}-1},&\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{2}-2}\\
\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{1}-1},&\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{2}-2}\end{smallmatrix}\big)\\
&\prod_{]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{stick})(\Scaff)}S_{\ell_{2}-\ell_{1}-1}(\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{1}-1},\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{2}-2})S_{\ell_{2}-\ell_{1}-1}(\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{1}-1},\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{2}-2})\\
&\prod_{\ell\in\ell(\Base)}r^{d}{\varepsilon}^{k_{\ell}}r^{-dk_{\ell}}\chi_{\alpha}(\xi_{2\ell-2},\omega_{\ell},\xi_{2\ell-1})\\
&\prod_{A\in P^{\prime}}(C|A|)^{2|A|}r^{d}\exp(-c|\frac{r}{|A|}\sum_{a\in A}q_{a}|^{0.99}){\mathbf{1}}(y_{A}\text{ is }r\text{-connected})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}[\Base].\end{split}

11.2.
The standard variable constraints

As in the proof of Proposition
4.10
, the integrand is easier to digest as a product
of constraints on each variable. The path variables are labelled by the set

(11.4)

\begin{split}\Lambda_{D}:=\{{\mlq\mlq p_{a}\mrq\mrq},{\mlq\mlq s_{a}\mrq\mrq}\}_{a\in K_{0}(\bm{\Gamma}[\Base])}\cup\{{\mlq\mlq y_{a}\mrq\mrq}\}_{a\in K(\bm{\Gamma}[\Base])}&\cup\{{\mlq\mlq\xi^{+}_{2\ell-2}\mrq\mrq},{\mlq\mlq\xi^{-}_{2\ell-2}\mrq\mrq},{\mlq\mlq\xi^{+}_{2\ell-1}\mrq\mrq},{\mlq\mlq\xi^{-}_{2\ell-1}\mrq\mrq}\}_{\ell\in\ell(\Base)}\\
&\cup\{{\mlq\mlq\xi^{+}_{0}\mrq\mrq},{\mlq\mlq\xi^{+}_{2N-1}\mrq\mrq}{\mlq\mlq\xi^{-}_{0}\mrq\mrq}{\mlq\mlq\xi^{-}_{2N-1}\mrq\mrq}\}\end{split}

We impose a total ordering
\leq_{\Lambda}
on
\Lambda
generated by the following rules:

•

For any
a,b\in K_{0}^{+}(\bm{\Gamma})
with
\sign(a)=+
and
\sign(b)=-
,

{\mlq\mlq p_{a}\mrq\mrq},{\mlq\mlq s_{a}\mrq\mrq},{\mlq\mlq y_{a}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq p_{b}\mrq\mrq},{\mlq\mlq s_{b}\mrq\mrq},{\mlq\mlq y_{b}\mrq\mrq}
.

•

If
a,b\in K_{0}^{+}(\bm{\Gamma})
satisfy
\sign(a)=\sign(b)
and either

\sign(a)=+
and
a\leq b
or
\sign(a)=-
and
a\geq b
, then

{\mlq\mlq y_{a}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq y_{b}\mrq\mrq}
,
{\mlq\mlq s_{a}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq s_{b}\mrq\mrq}
,
{\mlq\mlq p_{a}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq p_{b}\mrq\mrq}
.

•

Similarly, if
0\leq m\leq m^{\prime}\leq 2N-1
, then provided
{\mlq\mlq\xi^{+}_{m}\mrq\mrq},{\mlq\mlq\xi^{+}_{m^{\prime}}\mrq\mrq}\in\Lambda_{D}
,

{\mlq\mlq\xi^{+}_{m}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq\xi^{-}_{m}\mrq\mrq}
,

{\mlq\mlq\xi^{+}_{m}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq\xi^{+}_{m^{\prime}}\mrq\mrq}
,

{\mlq\mlq\xi^{-}_{m^{\prime}}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq\xi^{-}_{m}\mrq\mrq}
.

•

For any
a\in K_{0}^{+}(\bm{\Gamma})
,
{\mlq\mlq p_{a}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq s_{a}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq y_{a+1}\mrq\mrq}
,
and for any
\ell\in\Base(D)
,

{\mlq\mlq\xi^{+}_{2\ell-2}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq p_{\ell,+,0}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq s_{\ell,+,k_{\ell}}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq\xi^{+}_{2\ell-1}\mrq\mrq}
,

{\mlq\mlq\xi^{-}_{2\ell-1}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq p_{\ell,-,k_{\ell}}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq s_{\ell,-,0}\mrq\mrq}\leq{\mlq\mlq\xi^{-}_{2\ell-2}\mrq\mrq}
.

We are now ready to assign constraint functions
f_{\lambda}
for each label
\lambda\in\Lambda
,
much the same as we did in the calculation for Proposition
4.10
.
First we define the familiar momentum constraints

(11.5)

f_{{\mlq\mlq p\mrq\mrq}_{a}}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq p\mrq\mrq},a})=\begin{cases}r^{d}{\mathbf{1}}(|p_{\ell,+,0}-(\xi_{2\ell-2}^{+})_{p}|\leq\alpha r^{-1}),&a=(\ell,+,0)\\
r^{d}{\mathbf{1}}(|p_{\ell,-,k_{\ell}}-(\xi_{2\ell-1}^{-})_{p}|\leq\alpha r^{-1}),&a=(\ell,-,k_{\ell})\\
r^{d}\exp(-c(Nk_{max})^{-1}|r\sum_{a\in S}q_{a}|^{0.99}),&a=\max_{\leq_{\Lambda}}S\text{ for some }S\in P^{\prime}\\
(1+|q_{a}|)^{-20d}{\mathbf{1}}(||p_{a}|-|p_{+,0}||\leq\alpha\max\{|p_{0}|^{-1}s_{a}^{-1},r^{-1}\}),&\text{ else}.\end{cases}

The position constraints are slightly modified by the presence of the delta function

\delta(y_{a}^{+}-y_{a}^{-})
for each
a\in J^{+}_{clust}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
. For

a\in J^{+}_{clust}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
we define

f_{{\mlq\mlq y\mrq\mrq},a,-}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq y\mrq\mrq},a,-}):=r^{-d}\delta(y_{a}^{+}-y_{a}^{-}).

For the rest of the indices
a
we set

(11.6)

f_{{\mlq\mlq y\mrq\mrq},a}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq y\mrq\mrq},a}):=\begin{cases}r^{-d}{\mathbf{1}}(|y_{\ell,+,1}-((\xi^{+}_{2\ell-2})_{x}+s_{\ell,+,0}p_{\ell,+,0})|\leq\alpha r),&a=(\ell,+,1)\\
r^{-d}{\mathbf{1}}(|y_{\ell,-,k_{-}}-((\xi^{-}_{2\ell-1})_{x}-s_{\ell,-,k_{-}}p_{\ell,-,k_{-}})|),&a=(\ell,-,k_{\ell})\\
r^{-d}{\mathbf{1}}(|y_{\ell,+,j}-(y_{\ell,+,j-1}+s_{\ell,+,j-1}p_{\ell,+,j-1})|\leq\alpha r),&a=(\ell,+,j)\text{ for }j>1\\
r^{-d}{\mathbf{1}}(|y_{\ell,-,j+1}-(y_{\ell,-,j}+s_{\ell,-,j}p_{\ell,-,j})|\leq\alpha r),&a=(\ell,-,j)\text{ for }j<k_{\ell}.\end{cases}

The phase space position
{\mlq\mlq\xi\mrq\mrq}
constraints are now more complicated than the analogous calculation
in the proof of Proposition
4.10
due to the presence of the twinning functions
and ladder functions.

(11.7)

f_{{\mlq\mlq\xi\mrq\mrq},b,m}:=\begin{cases}d_{r}(\xi^{+}_{2\ell-1},(y_{\ell,+,k_{\ell}}+s_{\ell,+,k_{\ell}}p_{\ell,+,k_{\ell}},p_{\ell,+,k_{\ell}}))\leq N^{2}\alpha,&b=+,m=2\ell-1,\ell\in\Base\\
d_{r}(\xi^{-}_{2\ell-2},(y_{\ell,-,1}-s_{\ell,-,0}p_{\ell,-,0},p_{\ell,-,0}))\leq N^{2}\alpha,&b=-,m=2\ell-1,\ell\in\Base\\
M_{\ell_{2}-\ell_{1}-1}(\xi_{2\ell_{1}-1}^{+},\xi_{2\ell_{2}-2}^{+}),&b=+,\,\,\,]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{ladder})\\
d_{r}(U_{(\ell_{2}-\ell_{1})\tau}(\xi_{2(\ell_{1}-1)-1}^{+}),\xi_{2(\ell_{2}+1)-2}^{+})\leq N^{6},&b=+,m=2\ell_{2}-2,]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{stick})\\
T\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{1}-1},&\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{2}-2}\\
\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{1}-1},&\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{2}-2}\end{smallmatrix}\big),&b=-,m=2\ell_{2}-2,]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{ladder})\\
d_{r}(U_{(\ell_{2}-\ell_{1})\tau}\xi^{-}_{2(\ell_{1}-1)-1},\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{2}-2})\leq N^{6}&b=-,m=2\ell_{2}-2,]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{stick}).\end{cases}

The relevant fact we need about the maximal ladder functions is that

\sup_{\xi}\int M_{k}(\xi,\eta)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\eta\leq CN^{20},

which follows from Lemma
12.1
, which is proven in the next section.

The definitions of the
f_{\lambda}
functions are defined precisely so that the
integrand in (
11.3
) is bounded by the product of individual constraints.
That is,

\displaystyle\prod_{a\in J^{+}_{clust}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})}\delta(y_{a}^{+}-y_{a}^{-})\prod_{]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{ladder}(D))}M_{\ell_{2}-\ell_{1}-1}(\xi_{2\ell_{2}-1}^{+},\xi_{2\ell_{2}-2}^{+})T\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{1}-1},&\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{2}-2}\\
\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{1}-1},&\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{2}-2}\end{smallmatrix}\big)

\displaystyle\prod_{]\ell_{1},\ell_{2}[\in\ell({\mathcal{I}}^{stick})(\Scaff)}S_{\ell_{2}-\ell_{1}-1}(\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{1}-1},\xi^{+}_{2\ell_{2}-2})S_{\ell_{2}-\ell_{1}-1}(\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{1}-1},\xi^{-}_{2\ell_{2}-2})

\displaystyle r^{-d|K(\bm{\Gamma}_{D})|}\prod_{\ell\in\Base(D)}r^{d}\chi_{\alpha}(\xi_{2\ell-2},\omega_{\ell},\xi_{2\ell-1})\prod_{A\in P^{\prime}}r^{d}\exp(-c|\frac{r}{|A|}\sum_{a\in A}q_{a}|^{0.99})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq\prod f_{{\mlq\mlq p\mrq\mrq},a}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq p\mrq\mrq},a})f_{{\mlq\mlq y\mrq\mrq},a}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq y\mrq\mrq},a})\prod f_{{\mlq\mlq\xi\mrq\mrq},b,m}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq\xi\mrq\mrq},b}).

Thus, using the fact that the maximum cluster in the partition
P(\psi)
has
has cardinality bounded by
\|{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\|
to sum over the partitions
P^{\prime}
, we arrive at the bound

(11.8)

\begin{split}\|{\widetilde{X}}_{\Scaff,+}\|_{op}&\leq(CN)^{C\|{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\|}\sup_{\xi^{+}_{2N-1}}\max_{\psi^{+},\psi^{-}\in\Psi^{+}(\Scaff)}\max_{P^{\prime}\leq P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})}\\
&\qquad\int{\varepsilon}^{|K(\bm{\Gamma}[\Base])|}\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Gamma^{+}[\Base])\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Gamma^{-}[\Base])\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\prod f_{{\mlq\mlq p\mrq\mrq},a}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq p\mrq\mrq},a})f_{{\mlq\mlq y\mrq\mrq},a}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq y\mrq\mrq},a})f_{{\mlq\mlq\xi\mrq\mrq},b,m}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq\xi\mrq\mrq},b,m})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\prod_{A\in P^{\prime}}{\mathbf{1}}(y_{A}\text{ is }r\text{-connected})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}_{D}\\
&=:(CN)^{C\|{\mathcal{F}}\|}\max_{\psi^{+},\psi^{-}\in\Psi^{+}(\Scaff)}\max_{P^{\prime}\leq P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})}Y(\Scaff,\psi^{+},\psi^{-},P^{\prime}),\end{split}

where

(11.9)

\begin{split}Y(\Scaff,\psi^{+},\psi^{-},P^{\prime}):=\sup_{\xi^{+}_{2N-1}}&\int{\varepsilon}^{|K(\bm{\Gamma}[\Base])|}\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Gamma^{+}[\Base])\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Gamma^{-}[\Base])\\
&\qquad\qquad\prod f_{{\mlq\mlq p\mrq\mrq},a}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq p\mrq\mrq},a})f_{{\mlq\mlq y\mrq\mrq},a}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq y\mrq\mrq},a})f_{{\mlq\mlq\xi\mrq\mrq},b,m}(\bm{\Gamma}_{\leq{\mlq\mlq\xi\mrq\mrq},b,m})\\
&\qquad\qquad\prod_{A\in P^{\prime}}{\mathbf{1}}(y_{A}\text{ is }r\text{-connected})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bm{\Gamma}_{D}.\end{split}

Before we proceed we clarify the nature of the partitions involved in the above expression.
The first partition clearly present above is the partition
P^{\prime}
, which is some refinement
of
P(\psi^{+},\psi^{-})
. Due to the constraint that
y_{A}
is
r
-connected, we have

|y_{a}-y_{b}|\leq 4|A|r

for
(a,b)\in A\in P^{\prime}
. We can extend these constraints using the localizations from

\chi_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}^{+}
. In particular,
\chi^{+}_{{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}}
enforces

|y_{a}-y_{b}|\leq 4r

when either
a,b\in K^{+}_{all}
and
(a,b)\in F^{\prime}_{rec}
or
a,b\in K^{-}_{all}
and

(a^{*},b^{*})\in F^{\prime}_{rec}
. In either case we say
(a,b)\in F^{\prime}_{rec,+}
, meaning
that
(a,b)
is a recollision originally present in
\Gamma^{+}
and is now mirrored to

\Gamma^{-}
by our application of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. We also have

y_{a}=y_{a^{*}}
for
a\in J^{+}_{clust}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
.
Thus if
Q
is the coarsening of
P^{\prime}
which has
a\sim_{Q}b
when
(a,b)\in F^{\prime}_{rec,+}

and
a\sim_{Q}a^{*}
for
a\in J^{+}_{clust}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
, we can get a slightly better
